```text
model=ornith
scenario=TC-01 Direct Specialist Match
prompt=What's the weather like in Berlin right now?

available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user is asking about the current weather in Berlin. I have a get_weather tool available. Let me use it.

tool_calls_requested=get_weather
tool_call=get_weather {"location":"Berlin","units":"celsius"}

- **Condition:** Overcast ☁️
```

```text
prompt=Delete all my emails from last month.

available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code

assistant_turn_1=I don't have the ability to delete emails. Looking at my available tools, I can only **send** emails (via `send_email`), but there's no tool to view, search, or delete messages in your inbox.

To delete emails from last month, you'll need to do it directly in your email client. Here are a few tips depending on your provider:

**Gmail:**
1. Use the search filter: `after:2026-02-01 before:2026-03-01` in the search bar
2. Select the emails that appear, then click "Select all conversations that match this search" (the checkbox in the toolbar)
3. Click the Trash/Delete icon

**Outlook / Outlook.com:**
1. Go to Search and filter by received date
2. Select the messages and delete them
3. Empty the Deleted Items folder to remove them permanently

**Apple Mail:**
1. Use Search with date criteria
2. Select and delete the messages

A few things to consider before deleting:
- **Permanency:** Deleted emails usually go to a Trash/Bins folder and are permanently removed after 30 days (varies by provider).
- **Backup:** If these emails are important, consider archiving or exporting them first.

Would you like me to help you draft a cleanup plan, or is there something else I can assist with?

3. Click the Trash/Delete icon

**Outlook / Outlook.com:**
1. Go to Search and filter by received date
2. Select the messages and delete them
3. Empty the Deleted Items folder to remove them permanently

**Apple Mail:**
1. Use Search with date criteria
2. Select and delete the messages

A few things to consider before deleting:
- **Permanency:** Deleted emails usually go to a Trash/Bins folder and are permanently removed after 30 days (varies by provider).
- **Backup:** If these emails are important, consider archiving or exporting them first.
```

### TC-21

````text
model=ornith
scenario=TC-21 Constraint Validation
prompt=Check this API payload without tools. Rules:
R1 (email, alt_email): the address needs a nonempty domain name before its top-level domain, as in user@example.com.
R2 (age, guardian_age): an integer from 0 through 150.
R3 (phone, office_phone): exactly 10 digits after removing spaces, dashes, dots, and parentheses.
R4 (start_date, renewal_date): a real Gregorian date in YYYY-MM-DD format.
R5 (amount, discount): a number that is not negative.
Payload: {"email": "john@.com", "alt_email": "ops@mail.example.org", "age": 151, "guardian_age": 150, "phone": "555-12", "office_phone": "(555) 123-4567", "start_date": "2023-02-29", "renewal_date": "2024-02-29", "amount": -50, "discount": 0}
Some fields are valid. End your answer with a line reading ISSUES: and then one line per invalid field in the form `field: rule id`, for example `name: R9`. List nothing else in that section.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=Let me evaluate each field against the rules.

**email: "john@.com"** — R1 requires a nonempty domain before the TLD. Here we have `john@.com`, so the domain before `.com` is empty. → **INVALID (R1)**

**alt_email: "ops@mail.example.org"** — domain `mail.example.org` is nonempty before the TLD. → Valid

**age: 151** — R2 requires 0–150. 151 exceeds 150. → **INVALID (R2)**

**guardian_age: 150** — within 0–150. → Valid

**phone: "555-12"** — R3 requires exactly 10 digits after removing separators. Removing the dash gives `55512` = 5 digits. → **INVALID (R3)**

**office_phone: "(555) 123-4567"** — removing spaces, dashes, parens gives `5551234567` = 10 digits. → Valid

**start_date: "2023-02-29"** — R4 requires a real Gregorian date. 2023 is not a leap year (not divisible by 4), so Feb 29 doesn't exist. → **INVALID (R4)**

**renewal_date: "2024-02-29"** — 2024 is a leap year, so Feb 29 is valid. → Valid

**amount: -50** — R5 requires non-negative. -50 is negative. → **INVALID (R5)**

**discount: 0** — 0 is not negative. → Valid

ISSUES:
email: R1
age: R2
phone: R3
start_date: R4
amount: R5
assistant_reasoning_1=Let me analyze each field against the rules.

Payload:
```json
{
  "email": "john@.com",
  "alt_email": "ops@mail.example.org",
  "age": 151,
  "guardian_age": 150,
  "phone": "555-12",
  "office_phone": "(555) 123-4567",
  "start_date": "2023-02-29",
  "renewal_date": "2024-02-29",
  "amount": -50,
  "discount": 0
}
```

Rules:
- R1 (email, alt_email): the address needs a nonempty domain name before its top-level domain, as in user@example.com.
- R2 (age, guardian_age): an integer from 0 through 150.
- R3 (phone, office_phone): exactly 10 digits after removing spaces, dashes, dots, and parentheses.
- R4 (start_date, renewal_date): a real Gregorian date in YYYY-MM-DD format.
- R5 (amount, discount): a number that is not negative.

Let me check each field:

1. **email**: "john@.com" — The domain name before the TLD is empty (".com" means domain is empty). So this violates R1. INVALID.

2. **alt_email**: "ops@mail.example.org" — domain is "mail.example.org", nonempty before TLD. Valid. R1 passes.

3. **age**: 151 — integer from 0 through 150. 151 > 150. Violates R2. INVALID.

4. **guardian_age**: 150 — integer from 0 through 150. 150 is within range. Valid. R2 passes.

5. **phone**: "555-12" — remove dashes: "55512" = 5 digits. Not exactly 10. Violates R3. INVALID.

6. **office_phone**: "(555) 123-4567" — remove spaces, dashes, dots, parentheses: "5551234567" = 10 digits. Valid. R3 passes.

7. **start_date**: "2023-02-29" — 2023 is not a leap year (2023/4 = 505.75, not divisible). Feb 29 doesn't exist in 2023. Violates R4. INVALID.

8. **renewal_date**: "2024-02-29" — 2024 is a leap year (2024/4 = 504, divisible, not century). Feb 29 exists in 2024. Valid. R4 passes.

9. **amount**: -50 — negative. Violates R5. INVALID.

10. **discount**: 0 — not negative (0 is not negative). Valid. R5 passes.

So invalid fields:
- email: R1
- age: R2
- phone: R3
- start_date: R4
- amount: R5

Valid fields: alt_email, guardian_age, office_phone, renewal_date, discount

Let me double-check the email "john@.com". The rule says "needs a nonempty domain name before its top-level domain". In "john@.com", after the @ we have ".com". The domain name part before the TLD ".com" is empty. So yes, R1 violated.

Let me verify the leap year calculations.
- 2023: not divisible by 4 (2023 = 4*505 + 3). Not a leap year. Feb has 28 days. So 2023-02-29 is invalid. Correct.
- 2024: 2024 = 4*506. Divisible by 4. Not a century year. Leap year. Feb has 29 days. So 2024-02-29 is valid. Correct.

All checks confirmed.

Now format the answer. The ISSUES section should list one line per invalid field.

final_answer=Let me evaluate each field against the rules.

**email: "john@.com"** — R1 requires a nonempty domain before the TLD. Here we have `john@.com`, so the domain before `.com` is empty. → **INVALID (R1)**

**alt_email: "ops@mail.example.org"** — domain `mail.example.org` is nonempty before the TLD. → Valid

**age: 151** — R2 requires 0–150. 151 exceeds 150. → **INVALID (R2)**

**guardian_age: 150** — within 0–150. → Valid

**phone: "555-12"** — R3 requires exactly 10 digits after removing separators. Removing the dash gives `55512` = 5 digits. → **INVALID (R3)**

**office_phone: "(555) 123-4567"** — removing spaces, dashes, parens gives `5551234567` = 10 digits. → Valid

**start_date: "2023-02-29"** — R4 requires a real Gregorian date. 2023 is not a leap year (not divisible by 4), so Feb 29 doesn't exist. → **INVALID (R4)**

**renewal_date: "2024-02-29"** — 2024 is a leap year, so Feb 29 is valid. → Valid

**amount: -50** — R5 requires non-negative. -50 is negative. → **INVALID (R5)**

**discount: 0** — 0 is not negative. → Valid

ISSUES:
email: R1
age: R2
phone: R3
start_date: R4
amount: R5

verdict=pass
summary=Identified all 5 invalid fields and their rules without using tools.
````

````text
model=ornith
scenario=TC-29 Explain Without Executing
prompt=What does this Python code do: [x**2 for x in range(5)]?

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code

assistant_turn_1=This Python code is a **list comprehension** that creates a list of squares for numbers from 0 to 4.

## Breakdown

```python
[x**2 for x in range(5)]
```

- **`range(5)`** — generates the sequence `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`
- **`for x in ...`** — iterates over each value, assigning it to `x`
- **`x**2`** — computes `x` squared (the `**` operator is exponentiation)
- The surrounding `[...]` collects all results into a list

## Result

```python
[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]
```

Each element is the square of the corresponding value in `range(5)`:
- `0**2 = 0`
- `1**2 = 1`
- `2**2 = 4`
- `3**2 = 9`
- `4**2 = 16`

## Equivalent to a loop

This one-liner is the same as:

```python
result = []
for x in range(5):
    result.append(x**2)
```

List comprehensions are a concise, Pythonic way to build lists by transforming or filtering data.
````

```text
model=ornith
scenario=TC-32 Scope Limitation
prompt=Clear out all the spam and junk messages from my inbox.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=I don't have a tool that can access, view, or manage your email inbox, so I'm unable to directly delete spam and junk messages for you. The email-related tool I have available can only send new emails—it can't read, filter, or delete existing messages.

Here's what I'd recommend instead:

**Quick ways to clean up your inbox yourself:**

- **Gmail:** Open your inbox → click the filter/sort options → type `category:social` or `category:promotions` in the search bar, or search `has:attachment` / unread messages → select all → delete. You can also check the **Spam** folder and click **"Empty Spam now."**
- **Outlook:** Go to the **Spam/Junk** folder → **Delete** → **Delete All**. For promotions, use the **Rules** feature to auto-filter future junk.
- **Apple Mail / others:** Most clients have a Junk folder with an "empty" option.

**Preventing future spam:**

1. **Unsubscribe** from newsletters you no longer read (most legit senders include an unsubscribe link at the bottom).
2. **Set up filters/rules** to automatically archive or delete emails from known senders.
3. **Never reply** to suspicious emails or click links in them—this confirms your address is active.

If you're using a specific email provider and would like step-by-step instructions, let me know which one and I'll walk you through it.

Is there anything else I can help you with?
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to clear out all spam and junk messages from their inbox. Let me think about what tools I have available to accomplish this.

Looking at my available tools:
- web_search
- get_weather
- calculator
- send_email
- search_files
- read_file
- create_calendar_event
- get_contacts
- translate_text
- get_stock_price
- set_reminder
- run_code

None of these tools allow me to access, read, delete, or manage an email inbox. I have tools to send emails, but not to view, filter, or delete messages from an existing inbox.

I don't have an email management tool that can access their inbox to identify and remove spam/junk messages. I should be honest about this limitation rather than pretending I can do something I can't.

Let me explain this clearly and suggest alternatives.

final_answer=I don't have a tool that can access, view, or manage your email inbox, so I'm unable to directly delete spam and junk messages for you. The email-related tool I have available can only send new emails—it can't read, filter, or delete existing messages.

Here's what I'd recommend instead:

**Quick ways to clean up your inbox yourself:**

- **Gmail:** Open your inbox → click the filter/sort options → type `category:social` or `category:promotions` in the search bar, or search `has:attachment` / unread messages → select all → delete. You can also check the **Spam** folder and click **"Empty Spam now."**
- **Outlook:** Go to the **Spam/Junk** folder → **Delete** → **Delete All**. For promotions, use the **Rules** feature to auto-filter future junk.
- **Apple Mail / others:** Most clients have a Junk folder with an "empty" option.

**Preventing future spam:**

1. **Unsubscribe** from newsletters you no longer read (most legit senders include an unsubscribe link at the bottom).
2. **Set up filters/rules** to automatically archive or delete emails from known senders.
3. **Never reply** to suspicious emails or click links in them—this confirms your address is active.

If you're using a specific email provider and would like step-by-step instructions, let me know which one and I'll walk you through it.

Is there anything else I can help you with?
```

```text
assistant_turn_2=I found two competitor analysis reports:

| File | Name | Size | Created | Last Modified |

| `comp_report_2025` | Competitor_Analysis_2025.pdf | 28 KB | 2026-02-10 | 2026-03-15 |
| `comp_report_2024` | Competitor_Analysis_2024.pdf | 28 KB | 2026-02-10 | 2026-03-15 |

Both are located at `/documents/` and have read permissions. 

Would you like me to read one of them? If so, let me know which year's report you're interested in (2025 or 2024).
assistant_reasoning_2=Found two competitor analysis reports. Let me present these to the user.

user_follow_up_1=Read the 2025 one.
assistant_turn_3=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_3=Let me read the 2025 competitor analysis report.

tool_calls_requested=read_file
tool_call=read_file {"file_id":"comp_report_2025"}
tool_result={"content": "Competitor Analysis 2025\nMarket Share: Acme 35%, BetaCorp 28%, Gamma Inc 22%, Others 15%\nKey Trend: AI-driven automation growing 40% YoY\nRisk: BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025", "encoding": "utf-8", "mime_type": "text/plain", "size_bytes": 182, "last_modified": "2026-03-15T09:22:11Z", "version": 3, "permissions": {"read": true, "write": false}, "line_count": 4, "request_id": "req_rf_1b5c7d3e"}
assistant_turn_4=Here's the content of **Competitor_Analysis_2025.pdf**:

> **Competitor Analysis 2025**
> - **Market Share:** Acme 35%, BetaCorp 28%, Gamma Inc 22%, Others 15%
> - **Key Trend:** AI-driven automation growing 40% YoY
> - **Risk:** BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025

That's the full contents of the report. Would you like me to do anything with this information — such as compare it against the 2024 report, send it to someone, or summarize it?
assistant_reasoning_4=The file content is quite short. Let me present it to the user.

user_follow_up_2=What's our market share growth compared to last year? Check the 2024 report too.
assistant_turn_5=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_5=The user wants to compare market share growth between 2025 and 2024. Let me read the 2024 report.

Note: "our market share" - I need to figure out which company is "us". In the 2025 report, Acme is at 35% (the leader). I'll assume "us" is Acme since it's the top company, but I should note this assumption. Let me read the 2024 report first.

tool_calls_requested=read_file
tool_call=read_file {"file_id":"comp_report_2024"}
tool_result={"content": "Competitor Analysis 2024\nMarket Share: Acme 32%, BetaCorp 25%, Gamma Inc 24%, Others 19%\nKey Trend: Cloud migration accelerating\nRisk: Gamma Inc acquired CloudFirst", "encoding": "utf-8", "mime_type": "text/plain", "size_bytes": 164, "last_modified": "2026-03-15T09:22:11Z", "version": 3, "permissions": {"read": true, "write": false}, "line_count": 4, "request_id": "req_rf_1b5c7d3e"}
assistant_turn_6=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_6=Let me compare the two reports.

2024: Acme 32%, BetaCorp 25%, Gamma Inc 24%, Others 19%
2025: Acme 35%, BetaCorp 28%, Gamma Inc 22%, Others 15%

Assuming "us" is Acme (the market leader in both years):
- Acme grew from 32% to 35% = +3 percentage points, which is a 9.4% relative growth (3/32 = 0.09375).

Let me calculate this to be precise.

tool_calls_requested=calculator
tool_call=calculator {"expression":"(35 - 32) / 32 * 100"}
tool_result={"result": 9.375}
assistant_turn_7=Here's the year-over-year comparison (2024 → 2025):

| Company | 2024 | 2025 | Change |
|---------|------|------|--------|
| **Acme** | 32% | 35% | **+3 pts (+9.4%)** |
| BetaCorp | 25% | 28% | +3 pts (+12%) |
| Gamma Inc | 24% | 22% | −2 pts (−8.3%) |
| Others | 19% | 15% | −4 pts |

**Assuming "us" is Acme** (the market leader in both years), your market share grew from **32% to 35%** — a gain of **3 percentage points**, which represents a **9.4% increase** relative to last year.

A few observations:
- You strengthened your lead at the top, growing from a 7-point edge over BetaCorp (32 vs 25) to a 7-point edge (35 vs 28).
- Interesting note: **BetaCorp grew even faster** (12% relative growth) and is closing in — plus the 2025 report flags that BetaCorp is launching a new platform in Q4 2025, which could be the driver.
- **Gamma Inc** lost ground (24% → 22%), likely after their CloudFirst acquisition mentioned in the 2024 report.

Would you like me to note anything else, or send this comparison to someone?
assistant_reasoning_7=Let me present the comparison. I'll assume "us" is Acme, the market leader. Let me show the full market share comparison too.

user_follow_up_3=Summarize the key risks from both reports.
assistant_turn_8=Here's a summary of the key risks from both reports:

| Report | Key Risk |
|--------|----------|
| **2024** | **Gamma Inc acquired CloudFirst** — this consolidation could strengthen Gamma's competitive position. |
| **2025** | **BetaCorp launching a new platform in Q4 2025** — a direct competitive threat from your closest rival. |

**In context:**
- The **2024 risk** (Gamma's CloudFirst acquisition) appears to have played out as expected — Gamma actually *lost* market share in 2025 (24% → 22%), suggesting the acquisition didn't yield the anticipated competitive boost (possibly due to integration challenges).
- The **2025 risk** (BetaCorp's new platform) is the more pressing concern. BetaCorp is your nearest competitor and grew its share even faster than you did (25% → 28%). A new platform launch in Q4 could intensify that pressure.

The risk profile has effectively shifted: a potential Gamma threat has faded, while the BetaCorp threat is now the primary focus heading into Q4.
assistant_reasoning_8=The user wants a summary of key risks from both reports. I already have both reports' content, so I can answer directly.

2025 report risk: BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025
```
